# GM coverage

How complete each of the 128 GM patches is. Generated by
`examples/gm_coverage.py`, which reads the class column out of `patch_map`
so it cannot drift; the ratings live in that script and can be argued with.

| | meaning |
|---|---|
| **0** | nothing |
| **1** | general class |
| **2** | specific, theory |
| **3** | specific, theory + ear |
| **4** | specific, validated against a target |

3 means validated SEMANTICALLY BY EAR: it does what the instrument does, judged
by listening, whether or not by the same mechanism. 4 means validated against a
TARGET -- a recording of the instrument is one (fitted against it, or against a
family law measured on its close relatives); the hardware's own output of a
file is another (a Sound Canvas playing it, judged against ours by ear). Published measurements
that are not audio -- the Rhodes' high-speed-camera papers -- are better than
theory but cannot contradict the model the way a recording can, so those
voices sit at 2 and say so.

FOR A SYNTH PROGRAM THE INSTRUMENT IS A SYNTHESIZER. The 32 synth programs --
basses, strings, brass, leads, pads, effects and the synth drum -- are
patches on a Moog Messenger, and the Messenger
engine in `moog.py` is fitted against Ben's own Messenger, recorded through a
mixer and back (`examples/messenger_fit.py`): every knob law -- cutoff,
resonance, the contours and sustain, waveshape and sub wave, FM index, LFO 1
and MOD depths, the filter modes, the mixer, the noise and the output stage.
Whole notes, attack to release, agree within 0.6-2 dB rms on 28 of 32
programs. That is a recording of the instrument, so a pure Messenger patch
is a 4; one with a part no Messenger has (the vocal formants after it, the
guitars' valve, the MF-104M pedal, which is modelled and not recorded) is a 3.
And the reference runs the other way too: a patch's knobs are the hardware's,
so it plays on the Messenger itself.

## Where it stands

| rating | patches | share |
|---|---|---|
| 0 nothing | 0 | 0% |
| 1 general class | 0 | 0% |
| 2 specific, theory | 0 | 0% |
| 3 specific, theory + ear | 9 | 7% |
| 4 specific, validated against a target | 119 | 93% |

**No patch is played by a voice of the wrong physical kind.** The last three to be were 123 Bird Tweet, 124 Telephone Ring and 125 Helicopter, which are not recordings of the world but a chirp, a struck bell and a blade passing frequency.

## Channel 10: the percussion note map

59 notes, 35 to 87. Iowa's percussion pages carry cymbals, crotales and
hand percussion and **no drum kit at all** -- no snare, no bass drum, no
toms, which is 9630 of that collection's 12781 percussion notes. DrumGizmo's
DRSKit (CC-BY 4.0), a recorded kit, fills the kick, the snare, the toms and
the crashes hit hard; VSCO-2 CE and VCSL (CC0) the hand percussion, the
claps, the whistles and the second snare; Freesound takes the timbales, the
surdo (CC0) and the cuica (reinsamba, CC-BY).

| rating | notes | share |
|---|---|---|
| 3 specific, theory + ear | 2 | 3% |
| 4 specific, validated against a target | 57 | 97% |

## Drum sets: the program on a drum channel

The program on a drum channel is the drum SET, and a set changes only
some notes -- every blank in Roland's table is "same as Standard". The
Sound Canvas's sets on every map (SC-8850 owner's manual p.187): the
SC-55 map has 10 (and the CM-64/32L at 128), the SC-88 map 14, the
SC-88Pro map 25, the SC-8850 map 37. A set not built here plays
Standard. THE GOAL: every one built.

**Where it stands:** SC-8850 map 14 of 37, SC-88Pro map 14 of 25, SC-88 map 9 of 14, SC-55 map 10 of 10.

| 36 | 808 Bass Drum | `TR808Kick` | **4** | THE ROLAND TR-808, AS AN ENGINE (tr808.py: its two kinds of circuit as partials -- bridged-T resonators, and six square oscillators through band-passes -- every instrument a patch of knobs, read off the service notes' schematic where it fixes them). FITTED BY TWO LEVELS to Michael Fischer's recordings of one 808 (every knob at five positions): the GM set whose sample IS an 808 picks the setting, the patch fits that hit (examples/tr808_fit.py). Balanced within itself as GeneralUser's (examples/elkit_balance.py --kit=25). Ben: sounds incredible. The BASS DRUM: one network at 49.6 Hz, its first cycle at 4x (the time constant halved while the trigger lasts), the pitch sighing 0.5 oct onto it; its spectrum within 1-3 dB of the hit's to 8 kHz. Score 4.5 (BD0050) |

| 62 | Windchime | `SfxWindchime` | **3** | WINDCHIME: a metal chime tube struck, ringing out (Freesound, CC0): 13.0 dB -- one tube where GS's is a shaken set |
| 63 | Car-Engine | `SfxEngine` | **4** | CAR ENGINE fitted to a Freesound idle (CC0, examples/weather_fit.py): its firing at 47.4 Hz and harmonics as partials, each standing out of the engine's noise as the recording's (h1-h6 within 2 dB; those barely over the noise held at their measured heights), the noise's colour 1.6 dB rms |
| 64 | Car-Skid | `SfxSkid` | **4** | CAR SKID fitted to a Freesound handbrake turn (CC0): a squeal near 1.4 kHz over the tyre's roar (2.6 dB rms), never steady -- three near pitches beating under a slow swing, as the recording hops 53 cents rms |
| 65 | Car-Pass | `SfxCarPass` | **4** | CAR PASS fitted to a Freesound pass on a wet road (CC0): the tyres' noise (2.2 dB rms) swelling and falling, the engine one tone 18-21 dB over it (the recording's 16-20) falling 140 to 108 Hz through the pass -- GeneralUser GS's fast Doppler, an exponential in pitch, the noise riding it |
| 66 | Car-Crash | `SfxCrash` | **4** | CAR CRASH fitted to a Freesound crash with glass (CC0): its noise (0.2 dB rms) with glass clicks and metal rings as grains, holding 1.3 s and falling away over 0.6 s as the recording does |
| 67 | Siren | `SfxSiren` | **4** | SIREN from a Freesound fire engine (CC0): its measured series (a rotor's pulses) on a ROTOR (siren.py) fitted to the whole recorded gesture, 33 cents rms -- wound up while held (1.23 s), coasting after (11 s a factor e) and fading as it slows (its own fan: 1.1 dB rms); pressed again while it coasts it starts from the speed it has. Live steps the same model a callback at a time |
| 68 | Train | `SfxTrain` | **4** | TRAIN fitted to a Freesound freight train (CC0): rumble and the wheels' clacks (clusters), 0.2 dB rms; levelled to the jet, timidity playing GeneralUser GS's train as silence |
| 69 | Jetplane | `SfxJet` | **4** | JETPLANE fitted to a Freesound jet (CC0): its roar (0.0 dB rms), swelling in |

Not modelled: the noise loop's 7 Hz repetition. Levelled to the jet |

| 74 | Lasergun | `SfxLaser` | **3** | LASERGUN: a square wave swept down from 12x its note with a 0.1 s time constant, held and cut, as a Freesound blaster (CC0) measures. GeneralUser GS's is no reference (its envelope dies in 1 ms by the SF2; timidity plays a whine) |
| 75 | Explosion | `SfxExplosion` | **4** | EXPLOSION: a dynamite blast (Freesound, CC0; weather_fit: 0.4 dB), at once, held 0.6 s, dying over the next second as GeneralUser GS's does |
| 76 | Dog | `SfxDog` | **4** | DOG: a bark as a throat (examples/throat_fit.py): pulses at the six Freesound dogs' near-level contour (CC0; ~540 Hz, up 0.8 semitone, down 1.5), rough (6% jitter, 2 dB shimmer, breath), bright first then darkening ~9 dB at 4-8 kHz as the barks do, ringing their formants (LPC: 560 Hz, 1.35, 2.4, 3.5, 5.4 kHz) levelled to their bands |
| 77 | Horse-Gallop | `SfxHoof` | **3** | HORSE-GALLOP: horseshoe clicks (Ben's call) in GeneralUser GS's five-fall gallop -- a free bar's modes from 1.9 kHz gone in ~60 ms over a bright strike, set, not fitted (no recording has a shoe's click alone). Levelled to the footsteps: GeneralUser's swells in over 5 s, 20 dB down |
| 78 | Birds | `SfxBirds` | **4** | GM 123's bird (fitted), a one-shot gated 0.4 s |
| 79 | Rain | `SfxRain` | **4** | RAIN fitted to a Freesound recording (CC0, examples/weather_fit.py): the hiss band by band (1.7 dB rms) and the DROPS as clicks -- each a cluster of sines across 500 Hz-16 kHz at one instant (grains' ninth field), +16 dB over the hiss against the recording's +19, every octave lifted as its drops lift it. Ben: "the rain is good enough drops" |
| 80 | Thunder | `SfxThunder` | **4** | THUNDER fitted to a Freesound strike (CC0): its rumble under 250 Hz exact to 2 kHz, the cracks low grains; gated 1 s as GeneralUser GS's |
| 81 | Wind | `SfxWind` | **4** | WIND fitted to a Freesound howl (CC0): the 1 kHz howl over its rumble, 0.2 dB rms; one gust a key (a drum channel has no swell rows) |
| 82 | Sea Shore | `SfxSeashore` | **3** | GM 122's seashore, a one-shot gated 2.3 s -- by proxy, fitted to an ocean drum, not the sea |
| 83 | Stream | `SfxStream` | **4** | STREAM fitted to a Freesound brook (CC0): its hiss (0.2 dB rms), its drops a short ring at 2 kHz under clicks to 16 kHz -- the ring kept short (Ben: "a bit too bouncy ball") |
| 84 | Bubble | `SfxBubble` | **4** | BUBBLES set from a Freesound recording's own events (CC0): 11 a second at 300 Hz-1.2 kHz, each rising from 0.88 of its pitch (grains' glide), dying at 220 dB/s -- run as the bell tree's cascade, 22 rising (percussion_map.KIT_STROKES), as Ben approved it |
| 85 | Kitty | `SfxKitty` | **4** | KITTY (past the set, GS 123.4): a cat's meow as a throat (examples/throat_fit.py cat): peridactyloptrix's five meows (Freesound 214759, CC0), one 0.75 s syllable at 640 Hz, clean, near level then falling ~a semitone; LPC formants (on its harmonics, floored wide) with its fundamental and 4.5-5 kHz added, balanced to the meows (0.0 dB in every band). Ben: 'Kitty sounds good' |
| 86 | Growl | `SfxGrowl` | **3** | GROWL (past the set, GS 123.5): a dog's growl as a throat (throat_fit.py growl): qubodup's (Freesound 122183, CC0), one 0.75 s expression at ~130 Hz, its roughness measured (4.5% jitter: 35% voiced, the take's 39%), its MOUTH opening and closing (formants scaled per pulse, new: fitted to the jaw's path, 300-1000 over 80-300 Hz, 0.9 dB rms), its LIFE a level contour (new: up in 30 ms, a sagging body, a 0.15 s release), balanced to the bursts (0.0 dB). Ben: 'good enough' -- a player's pitch bend gives it its character |

### 127 CM-64/32L

| # | note | class | | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 35 | Acoustic Bass Drum | `KickDrum` | **4** | ACOUSTIC BASS DRUM -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 35: DRSKit's kick (DrumGizmo, CC-BY 4.0), 29 strokes on its two close mics: eleven measured modes, damped like a pillowed kick (head modes 40-130 dB/s, fitted by BAND so two modes 7% apart are not each read twice), a stroke-scaled glide of 150-350 cents, the beater's click. Two semitones under 36, at 39.4 Hz. Ben, by ear: sounds deeper |
| 36 | Acoustic Bass Drum | `KickDrum` | **4** | ACOUSTIC BASS DRUM -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 36: as 35, at the recorded kick's own 44.4 Hz |
| 37 | Rim Shot | `SideStick` | **4** | RIM SHOT -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 37: DRSKit's cross-stick, fitted as the measured snare struck on the rim: its head under the stick, the rim's click at 1.05 and 1.93 kHz, wires that die faster. Level by ear, 3 dB under the snare (the recorded 20.6 left it buried under the kick) |
| 38 | Acoustic Snare Drum | `AcousticSnare` | **4** | ACOUSTIC SNARE DRUM -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 38: DRSKit's snare (29 strokes, top and bottom mics): the head at 196 Hz with a tom's modes, choked in 50 ms; the wires a buzz of jittered noise in third-octave bands, fitted on band, onset balance and flatness (a chord of sines at the right band levels was not a snare; neither was a comb in phase -- "pew pew"). Needed the renderer's onsets to start on their own sample. Ben, by ear: sounds so much better |
| 39 | Hand Clap | `HandClap` | **4** | HAND CLAP -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 39: VCSL claps (CC0, examples/clap_fit.py): an ensemble -- seven hands flammed over 65 ms, each a fitted noise burst. Ben: sounds great |
| 40 | Electronic Snare Drum | `SimmonsSnare` | **4** | ELECTRONIC SNARE DRUM -- the MT-32's, as the Electronic set's 38: the SDS-V's snare module on the same engine: one oscillator near 450 Hz under its noise, fitted to the real Simmons snare GeneralUser's is closest to (hit 36), score 7.6 -- its decay within a few ms of the hit's. The two-tone snare fitted to GeneralUser itself stays in the code (ElectronicSnareProperties) |
| 41 | Acoustic Low Tom | `FloorTom` | **4** | ACOUSTIC LOW TOM -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 41: DRSKit's floor tom, 30 strokes on its close mic: modes 1 : 1.631 : 2.012 : 2.349 : 2.971, a long ring (the fundamental 13 dB/s, about 2.5 s), a two-stage decay, the stick's click, a glide that grows with the stroke. Ben, by ear |
| 42 | Closed High Hat | `ClosedHiHat` | **4** | CLOSED HIGH HAT -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 42: Iowa hi-hat, five takes |
| 43 | Acoustic Low Tom | `FloorTom` | **4** | ACOUSTIC LOW TOM -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 43: as 41, higher |
| 44 | Open High Hat 2 | `OpenHiHat` | **4** | OPEN HIGH HAT 2 -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 46: Iowa hi-hat, open |
| 45 | Acoustic Middle Tom | `TomTom` | **4** | ACOUSTIC MIDDLE TOM -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 45: DRSKit's rack tom, 27 strokes on its close mic: modes 1 : 1.477 : 1.755 : 2.259, the stick's click 30-60 dB under the fundamental to 6 kHz, a glide that grows with the stroke. Ben, by ear |
| 46 | Open High Hat 1 | `OpenHiHat` | **4** | OPEN HIGH HAT 1 -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 46: Iowa hi-hat, open |
| 47 | Acoustic Middle Tom | `TomTom` | **4** | ACOUSTIC MIDDLE TOM -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 47: as 45, higher |
| 48 | Acoustic High Tom | `HighTom` | **4** | ACOUSTIC HIGH TOM -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 48: as 45, higher |
| 49 | Crash Cymbal | `CrashCymbal1` | **4** | CRASH CYMBAL -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 49: Iowa 17" suspended crash for the modes, refitted to DRSKit's left crash hit HARD with the stick's shank (11 strokes over 37 dB): the cascade's swell of 2-8 kHz after the strike, scattered (in order it glided), growing with the stroke; a slow ring at its own 8.5 dB/s; the shank's thud. Ben, by ear: sounds great |
| 50 | Acoustic High Tom | `HighTom` | **4** | ACOUSTIC HIGH TOM -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 50: as 45, higher |
| 51 | Ride Cymbal | `RideCymbal` | **4** | RIDE CYMBAL -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 51: Iowa 21" ride, bow |
| 54 | Tambourine | `Tambourine` | **4** | TAMBOURINE -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 54: Iowa's tambourines, fitted: 77 measured jingle modes in four clusters, the hand's slap on the head, and each stroke catching the jingles differently (alike, a run of strokes rang metallic) |
| 56 | Cowbell | `CowbellStroke` | **4** | COWBELL -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 56: VSCO-2 CE (CC0) cowbell, fitted: 28 measured modes each at its own rate, clean; the strike dense and noisy from 400 Hz, its attack contrast matched. Ben: better than the first fit, which was jittery and modal |
| 60 | Hi Bongo | `BongoHigh` | **4** | HI BONGO -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 60: VSCO-2 CE (CC0) high bongo (161 Hz), fitted: measured modes ringing clean, the slap a dense noisy strike, strokes varying as the takes do |
| 61 | Low Bongo | `BongoLow` | **4** | LOW BONGO -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 61: as 60, the low bongo (142 Hz) |

| 64 | Low Conga | `CongaLow` | **4** | LOW CONGA -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 64: VSCO-2 CE (CC0) conga, the middle of its three (164 Hz), open at three dynamics |
| 65 | High Timbale | `TimbaleHigh` | **4** | HIGH TIMBALE -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 65: hello_flowers' timbales (freesound 41196, CC0), the high drum's ten free strokes cut from 50 hits (examples/freesound_slice.py): 46 measured modes ringing to 3.8 kHz, 224 Hz; fitted to 5.1 dB. The strike dense, jittered and SCATTERED in its bands (click_scatter): on an even grid its partials beat in a sweep -- Ben: "a phaser in the initial attack" |
| 66 | Low Timbale | `TimbaleLow` | **4** | LOW TIMBALE -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 66: as 65, the low drum's nine (186 Hz, 33 modes), fitted to 6.3 dB |

| 68 | Low Agogo | `AgogoLow` | **4** | LOW AGOGO -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 68: VSCO-2 CE (CC0) agogo bell 3 (696 Hz), as 67 |
| 69 | Cabasa | `Cabasa` | **4** | CABASA -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 69: VSCO-2 CE (CC0) cabasa as SHAPED NOISE: measured, a twist is noise (its grain is its own spectrum with random phases), so the recording's third-octave spectrum as noise rows under the fitted swell, shake and 0.33 s settle -- colour within 0.3 dB, envelope 3.0. The impact burst before it clicked where the takes do not. Ben: sounds great |
| 70 | Maracas | `Maracas` | **4** | MARACAS -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 70: VSCO-2 CE (CC0) maracas as SHAPED NOISE, fitted to the SINGLE strokes (two of the five files are two strokes, one three): the recording's spectrum as noise rows, a swell, the shake, a 0.29 s settle falling 40 dB -- colour within 0.8 dB but for the gourd's 2.3 kHz cliff, envelope 3.2. A little seed-click grain above 4 kHz is the recording's and not ours. Ben: sounds great |
| 71 | Short Whistle | `ShortWhistle` | **4** | SHORT WHISTLE -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 71: VCSL ball whistle, short blast (CC0, examples/whistle_fit.py): one tone at 2.7 kHz, the pea swinging it 144 cents at 40 Hz and chopping it, a 0.19 s blast; the air above the tone only. Ben: sounds good |
| 72 | Long Whistle | `LongWhistle` | **4** | LONG WHISTLE -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 72: as 71, the long blast (0.89 s, 80 cents at 30 Hz) |
| 73 | Quijada | `Vibraslap` | **4** | QUIJADA -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 58: VSCO-2 CE (CC0) vibraslap: the rod's swing at 27.4 Hz striking both sides, the far one dropping out as the swing dies (the roll slowing, 55 strikes a second to 27); the pins' clatter as strike noise, within 1 dB of the takes from 900 Hz up. Score 2.34 (was 2.72). Ben: sounds great now |
| 75 | Claves | `Claves` | **4** | CLAVES -- the MT-32's, as Standard's 75: Iowa claves, three pairs |
| 76 | Laughing | `SfxLaugh` | **4** | the CM-32L's, as the SFX set's 52: LAUGHING: a "ho ho ho" (Ben's call; a woman's laugh never read as one) -- a throat pulsed as a Freesound Santa says it (CC0): four arcing "ho"s and a long falling "hooo", each glottal pulse ringing his /o/'s formants (LPC: 500, 850 Hz, 2.5, 2.9, 5.7 kHz; examples/throat_fit.py), so the pitch slides through a fixed vowel. 2.1 s where GeneralUser GS's laugh is 0.9 |

| 79 | Heartbeat | `SfxHeartbeat` | **3** | the CM-32L's, as the SFX set's 55: HEART BEAT: not a recording -- the heart's own waveform, a textbook ECG beat's Fourier series, 1 Hz harmonics at the FFT's gains and phases (examples/ecg_heart.py: rendered, correlation 1.000) -- and over it the BLOOD FLOW as a Doppler hears it, noise filling to a top that follows an artery's velocity (3 kHz at peak; systole, the dicrotic notch, diastole's run-off). Levels held: VCSL's are normalized. The undamped strings ring sympathetically (the free-string register) |

A small board's brightness, which GM's name asks for; the grand voiced hard is kept as a voicing. Ben: very close, then great once the attack's knee went in |
| 2 | Electric Grand Piano | `ElectricGrand` | **4** | a CP-70, FITTED TO PIANOBOOK'S "DAVID'S CP70" (Conner Sawchuk; 31 keys, 4-7 layers at their recorded levels, measured only; examples/upright_fit.py --ref=cp70): the short strings' 1/L^4 stiffness, derived, held (F1's B 12.6e-4 recorded, 12.0 derived); no soundboard, a piezo on the bridge; the decay fitted -- a fundamental that sings over fast upper partials (8.6 -> 6.8 dB); the hammers' brightness swinging far with velocity, dark soft and bright hard (13.4 -> 10.3 dB); the register's level (4.3 -> 3.5). The rest is one instrument's key-by-key variation. Ben: sounds great |
| 3 | Honky-tonk Piano | `HonkyTonk` | **3** | the measured upright (GM 1) with the tuner's hand off: unisons 8-20 cents, CC1 the wheel. The upright is fitted; the detune is judged from beat rates, not measured |
| 4 | Electric Piano 1 | `Rhodes` | **4** | FITTED TO LEARMAN'S 1977 MARK I off the harp connector (jRhodes3d, CC BY-NC, measured only; examples/rhodes_fit.py), on what his EQ cancels -- the growl a harder layer adds, the hard attack's first 50 ms, the bark's fade, the decay (a tine sings 3-4x longer than we had it), the bass h1 BUILDING for seconds (F1 peaks 3.9 s in): the paper's own pickup (DAFx-17), a point tip over the magnet's frustum face on an arc, so a wide bass swing spikes each half cycle; each harmonic a sum of decaying layers, the swing's power series, at its true phase. Then voiced by ear: a forte that buzzes in the bass, mf untouched, and the buzz dying away faster than the tone (a wide swing damps faster); the register level even across the keyboard, as Gwazda's Wurlitzer measures (it had run 43 dB C2-C7 riding the swing). Ben, live: good and distinct from the Wurlitzer |
| 5 | Electric Piano 2 | `Wurlitzer` | **4** | FITTED TO GWAZDA'S BALTIWURLI, a 200A (measured only; examples/wurli_fit.py), DI and speaker sets, the takes the recording's clipper spared: the reed swings through a SLOT in the plate (Pfeifle & Muenster, DAFx-17), a square-edged bump, not the one-sided 1/d first read (ladder h2-h32 14.6 -> 10.8 dB rms) -- centred it loses the fundamental; each harmonic's envelope a signed sum of exponentials, so a wide bass swing buzzes to h32 against the edges and its fundamental builds back as the swing decays (the lingering low attack, Ben); each layer at its true phase in the wave (in phase, a flat top came out lopsided); the reed's decay by register (log-rate 0.35); the action's thump from the Mech set, at the pack's own mix; the register level to the DI's own (6.2 -> 2.1 dB rms). Not modelled: a fixed ring near 387 Hz from E5 up. Ben: better on the low buzz; kept; live, good and distinct from the Rhodes |
| 6 | Harpsichord | `Harpsichord` | **4** | FITTED TO VCSL'S HARPSICHORDS (CC0; examples/harpsi_fit.py): the English Normal and Lute sets and the Flemish High and Low, each stop measured as its difference from the 8' on one instrument, so the room drops out. A STRING BANK, like the piano: every choir on a key is present, each slightly apart in cents, stiffness and partial tuning, and the stops (the CC11 word, rated below) choose which the jacks pluck; a key lifts every damper, so the undrawn choirs ring through the bridge. The quill plucks at fixed force, so the comb never fills and there are no dynamics. The ladder and body over the whole band, partials to 16 kHz, the decay by band (log-rate error 0.96 -> 0.65), random partial phases (in phase they swept like a phaser, Ben), and the quill's catch before it slips (3.5-6 ms, measured). Ben: sounds great |
| 7 | Clavinet | `Clavinet` | **4** | FITTED TO A HOHNER D6 (Aalto's companion page to Gabrielli, Valimaki & Bilbao, ICMC 2011: Bb2, E4, F#5, each twice, measured only; examples/clav_fit.py) on the DAFx-12 pickup paper's geometry -- a 67.8 cm string at 161 Hz where the estimate had 0.26 m, the bridge pickup at 4 cm and the centre one 18.5-6.5 cm from the termination, the four switch settings (centre, bridge, both, anti-phase) -- the takes on the CENTRE pickup (6.7 dB rms, against 7.6 for the bridge); the ladder darkening up the keyboard (F#5's h2 25 dB down where the estimate had it level), a string that sings (Bb2 2-3 dB/s, not the estimate's 17), B 3.5e-5 measured (not 1.1e-4); and the KEY-UP, measured: a thump at the mechanism's fixed Hz plus the string let go at its own 2nd-4th harmonics, 35 dB under the strike and gone in ~30 ms -- live as well, stamped at the key-up. Three notes: the compass past them is the length law's. Ben: sounds good |

Its sympathetic strings tuned on Sa; Indian intonation as a secondary tuning (ragas.py: just, Pythagorean, equal, Bel's Todi, 27 ragas measured from Saraga) and a meend that locks the wheel to the raga's swaras. ## Stops: the registers within a program

A program's rating is its default registration's. Each stop of a
registerable program is its own claim, rated on the same scale; the
names are the code's (`stop_ranks`), and the bit is the stop word's.
Controls are the rest of the console that are not stops.

### 6 Harpsichord -- the harpsichord's registers (CC11 stop word)

| bit | stop | | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | **4** | the lower 8': VCSL's English harpsichord over the whole band -- ladder, body, decay as m^0.6, partials to 16 kHz, the quill's catch before it slips -- and a string among three: the key's other choirs answer through the bridge (register.py), each its own cents and wire |
| 1 | 8-upper | **4** | the Harpsimachord (Pianobook, a Kirckman copy), its Upper against its Lower: within +-3 dB at every harmonic, so plucked where the lower 8' is (0.115; the Flemish pair's ratio, 0.083, was 13.1 dB rms off, now 3.1) and dying 1.12x as fast (11 keys). Plucks ~3 ms after the lower 8' (asserted: no coupled recording) |
| 2 | 4 | **4** | the Harpsimachord's Lower+4 against its Lower, the 4' read on the 8''s even harmonics against the odd ones beside them: plucked at 0.21 (twice the 8''s fraction), darker, dying faster (2.89 -> 1.62 dB rms); level 0.57 from the takes' totals (+0.9 dB), not the ladder's 1.05 -- Ben, A/B: "Levelled sounds more natural". Its own bridge (half the coupling, asserted) |
| 3 | lute | **4** | the Harpsimachord's Buff against its Lower (VCSL's English Lute before it): a gentler buff, the treble 6-10 dB down at h3-h12 and the bass untouched (7.18 -> 2.20 dB rms), dying 1.62x as fast as the 8' (early partials); its tail is the other 8' answering, 5.7 cents off (VCSL's 5.8), through the register VCSL's tail fitted; the pad damps choir A's string whichever jack plucks it. Ben, A/B: "After lute sounds more natural" |

|---|---|---|---|

| 3 | 4 | **4** | as 8: both packs, 3.4-3.6 dB |

| 6 | 1-3/5 | **4** | the L102, as 16: level with the 2' and 1.3 dB over the 1-1/3' on their wheels (0.52 -> 0.59); its wheel the tempered seventeenth, read 11.4 cents sharp |
| 7 | 1-1/3 | **4** | the rok pack, 4.0 dB |
| 8 | 1 | **4** | the rok pack, 3.1 dB, with the foldback above the 91st wheel |
| 9 | perc 3rd L102 | **4** | the L102's third-harmonic tap, recorded alone on the fast decay: 90 dB/s, -6.6 dB against the 8' on the same wheel (its rise with pitch, +4.5 dB/octave, is the spinet's circuit: left flat) |
| 10 | perc 2nd L102 | **4** | the L102's second, as its third: 90 dB/s, -14.4 dB |

| 12 | perc 2nd B3 | **4** | Real Rotor Organ's second, as its third: +6.3 dB over its 8' |
| -- | Leslie slow (chorale) | **4** | the tonewheel pack: the bass rotor's lobe from 160 Hz, the cabinet's response, the crossover's shimmer |
| -- | Leslie fast (tremolo) | **4** | the rok pack: the horn's throw and lobe refitted, 6.9 -> 4.7 dB |
| -- | key click and leakage | **4** | every drawbar's contact at its own instant over 15 ms, the bounce's noise, the unpulled wheels at -48 dB: within ~1 dB of the takes |
| -- | percussion (GM 17) | **3** | the four tap stops (perc 3rd/2nd, L102/B3), each a rank decaying on its own, fitted; NOT modelled, the B3's own behaviour: its single trigger (keyed only from every key up), its 1' cancel while on, its normal-volume duck of the drawbars |
| -- | overdrive (GM 18) | **4** | the rok pack's energy off the drawbars' harmonics: to the edge of breakup, drive 0.92 |
| -- | scanner vibrato/chorus (CC94: off, V1-V3, C1-C3) | **3** | the late B-3's circuit SOLVED (scanner.py; Werner, Dunkel & Germain, DAFx-16, its Table 1): the LC ladder's 19 taps by AC nodal analysis -- lowpass, dispersion, standing-wave ripple, the tapered early taps -- and on chorus the bottom rail's 0.71 of the signal undelayed under the scanned ~0.3 (Rc 22 k; the service manual's 'part of the signal across the line, the rest across a resistor in series'); the scanner's crossfade over its 16 plates at 6.87 Hz, one motor for every key: a delay row per band, each partial's mean gain, and the crossfade's own motion as sidebands within 40 dB of their partial and 60 of its note (Ben, A/B: 'Tier B sounds better'). Live = file to 0.15 dB. No recording of a B-3's scanner to fit against |

### 19 Church Organ -- the church organ's console (28-bit stop word, CC11 | CC43<<7 | CC44<<14 | CC45<<21)

| bit | stop | | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | principal 8 | **4** | Pitea's HV Principal 8: its own series at three anchor pitches, its speech and overshoot fitted register by register, its wind. Ben: sounds great |
| 1 | octave 4 | **4** | Pitea's HV Oktava 4: its own series, brighter over the middle keys than the 8' at the same pitch; the speech the 8''s law at its pitch |
| 2 | super octave 2 | **4** | Pitea's HV Oktava 2: its own series, near-pure in the treble (h2 33 dB down) |
| 3 | quint 2-2/3 | **4** | Pitea's Positiv Kvinta 2 2/3: its own series at its pitch, its speech the principal's law at its own pitch (its overshoot on its own harmonics, not the key's) |
| 4 | principal 16 | **4** | FITTED ON BORROWED PIPES (examples/pitea_newranks.py --hold-level): Pitea has no open 16' on a manual, so its series is the Principal 8's pipes an octave down and, under C3, the pedal Oktava 8's (open, down to C1) -- within ~2-3 dB of the 8''s series it had read at its pitch, and its bottom octave now measured; its level held. Ben, A/B: approved |
| 5 | quint 5-1/3 | **4** | FITTED ON BORROWED PIPES (examples/pitea_newranks.py --hold-level): Pitea has no 5 1/3 (its pedal mixture's quint is the plein-jeu's 2 2/3), so its series is the Kvinta 2 2/3's pipes an octave down, which sound its pitch, from C3 -- h2-h10 6-10 dB under the principal series it had borrowed, a quint that FUSES into the chorus; its level held where the console had it. Ben, A/B: blends better |
| 6 | flute 8 | **4** | Pitea's Positiv Gedakt 8 (examples/pitea_stops.py): its own series at three anchor pitches, read at the rank's pitch (h3 12-20 dB down, the evens 30-50); its speech fitted (24 of its own periods, no overshoot -- it had taken the principal's); its wind fitted before. Level held |
| 7 | mixture III | **4** | Pitea's Huvudverk Mixtur IV, its harmonics read key by key: the composition breaking back at every C (1 1/3 1 2/3, then 2 1 1/3 1, 2 2/3 2 1 1/3, 4 2 2/3 2 -- the IV's lowest three), each pipe the principal's measured series at its own pitch; within 2-5 dB of the IV's three, its fourth rank absent |
| 8 | reed 8 | **4** | Pitea's Positiv Cromorne 8: its series -- the evens there, which the odd-only reed had not -- its speech (12 periods, no overshoot), its wind and attack rush fitted before. Heard as BuxWV 140's fugue reed |
| 9 | reed 16 | **4** | the extended set's Basson 16, as reed 8. Heard as BuxWV 161's Posaune |
| 10 | reed 4 | **4** | Pitea's Svallverk Clarion 4, as reed 8: bright to h7, speaking slowly (18 periods over an 80 ms valve) |
| 11 | trumpet 8 | **4** | Pitea's Huvudverk Trumpet 8, as reed 8: its own series in place of the orchestral trumpet's, which a forced stiffness had cut off above 2.6 kHz; speech 18 periods. Trimmed by ear in BuxWV 161 |
| 12 | bourdon 16 | **4** | Pitea's Huvudverk Gedackt Pommer 16, as the flute 8: its series, its speech (12 periods, the upper harmonics a little ahead) |

| -- | pipe speech and release | **4** | fitted on the Principal 8 by register; every flue rank takes the law at its own pitch |
| -- | shared wind (the chest) | **3** | every pipe on the chest hears the others' demand: a chord dips the pressure as it enters and surges it as it lifts, a reservoir ringing at 4 Hz and a regulator restoring it -- the form the physics' (Pykett, Carlsson 2002, the Rodgers wind patent), the size Ben's ear's (flexibility 1 of off/1/2.5/8: a five-note chord takes ~2 cents from a held note). No recording of one organ's coupling was fitted: it varies organ to organ. On by default |
| -- | the key's groove | **2** | not modelled: the stops on a key share its channel, so a release drains faster with more drawn, and the pipes go flat as it drains (Pitea's reeds 35-68 cents at C3-C4) |

### 20 Reed Organ -- the harmonium's registers (CC43/44/45 stop word: bass | treble at E4/F4)

| bit | stop | | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | cor anglais 1 | **4** | the bass 8' foundation: donyaquick's harmonium, 32 keys, the series within 2.5 dB rms |
| 1 | bourdon 2 | **4** | the bass 16': the Debain's Bourdon (Silvestre's set), its difference from the Debain's foundation key by key carried onto ours as tables at three anchors (the takes are low-passed and denoised: each read over its live band), its slow speech (36 cycles) and its level by the set's balance (examples/debain_registers.py). Ben, examples/harmonium_registers_demo.py: they sound great |
| 2 | clairon 3 | **4** | the bass 4': its series and speech the Debain's Clairon recorded alone (as bourdon 2), its level and its cents (each key 0.4-12 sharp of its octave) donyaquick's 8'+4' take's -- where the 8'+4' had fitted its brightness only as a sum: against that take the evens h2-h8 now within 2.1 dB (were 5.0), h10-h12 5-9 dB dark (the Debain's cut) |
| 3 | basson 4 | **4** | the bass 8' solo: the Debain's Basson, as bourdon 2. Ben, examples/harmonium_registers_demo.py: they sound great |
| 4 | flute 1 | **4** | the treble 8' foundation, as cor anglais 1 |
| 5 | clarinette 2 | **4** | the treble 16': the Debain's Clarinette, HOLLOW at last (h3 over h2: the cavity the flow model could not make), as bourdon 2. Ben, examples/harmonium_registers_demo.py: they sound great |
| 6 | fifre 3 | **4** | the treble 4', as clairon 3 (the Debain's Fifre, its top thirteen keys broken: those carry the keys below) |
| 7 | hautbois 4 | **4** | the treble 8' solo: the Debain's Hautbois, as bourdon 2. Ben, examples/harmonium_registers_demo.py: they sound great |
| 8 | celeste | **4** | the Debain's Voix celeste 16: TWO ROWS beating on their own, 10.5-18.6 cents apart key by key (median 15.4; every harmonic's swing at m times one rate), its series as bourdon 2. Ben, examples/harmonium_registers_demo.py: they sound great |
| 9 | expression | **2** | the reservoir shut, the feet the wind: brightness with pressure, asserted |
| 10 | percussion | **2** | a hammer on the front 8' reeds at key-down, asserted |
| 11 | tremolo | **4** | the Debain's Tremolo (its Musette with the flutter on): a level swing at 9.52 Hz, depth 0.25, ours on the Musette reading the same; it flutters every reed drawn, the Debain's only its one row. Ben, examples/harmonium_registers_demo.py: they sound great |
| 12 | violoncelle | **4** | the Debain's Violoncelle 8', bass (bit 12): added from its takes, as bourdon 2. Ben, examples/harmonium_registers_demo.py: they sound great |
| 13 | sourdine | **4** | the Debain's Sourdine 8', bass (bit 13), the muted stop 10.3 dB under the foundation: added, as bourdon 2. Ben, examples/harmonium_registers_demo.py: they sound great |
| 14 | musette | **4** | the Debain's Musette 16', treble (bit 14): added, as bourdon 2. Ben, examples/harmonium_registers_demo.py: they sound great |

## Variations: the GS banks (CC0)

A GS bank select (CC0 = bank, then the program) picks a variation tone
under a program (SC-55 owner's manual p.68). 175 of the SC-55's 175 are built,
and 142 of the SC-88 map's (Roland's SC-88Pro manual; gs_inventory.md);
one not built plays its capital tone, as a Sound Canvas does.

Levels the robust numbers only: the Fender is a different guitar from the steel-string's fit. | 104.8 | SC-55 | Tambra | `Tambra` | **3** | Roland's other tanpura (one voice to the Tamboura's two; no manual says more, no recording of either tone here): ours is the same tanpura with its jawari SET ANOTHER WAY, fitted to sankalp's take 1 (CC BY 4.0; examples/tanpura_fit.py --take=1 --cls=TambraProperties), whose cells differ from take 2's by 9-13 dB -- the brighter setting, the top over 5 kHz ~13 dB up, the swirl centred at 5.4 kHz and stronger; 10.1 dB rms over 250 cells (4-5 plucks a string). At the sitar's level. Ben: 'Sounds good' |
| 104.16 | SC-55 | Tamboura | `Tamboura` | **4** | the tanpura, fitted to sankalp's drone takes (Freesound pack 9571, CC BY 4.0; examples/tanpura_fit.py): no string rings alone in them, so each is read through the partials that are its alone -- Pa's odd harmonics, the low Sa's 1st, 5th, 7th, 11th ... -- from each pluck to its next, ~14 plucks a string. The jawari's SWIRL as a burst in its band (bloom_decay_dbs, new): ~1.5-4.5 kHz rising 10-20 dB to a peak 0.3-0.85 s in and falling at 21 dB/s, a fundamental 15-20 dB under it, the decay's register slope from the two strings' own rates (9.6 and 6.0 dB/s); 9.6 dB rms over 241 cells (one take: the two disagree by 9-13 dB, two settings of the thread). The sitar's jawari model without its taraf; at the sitar's level. Ben: 'Sounds great' |
| 105.1 | SC-88 | Muted Banjo | `Banjo_sc88_105_1` | **4** | SC-88 map: GM 105 Banjo with decay_db=30.0, tonal_dampening=1.2 |
| 105.8 | SC-55 | Rabab | `Rabab` | **3** | the Afghan rubab on the banjo's drumhead, gut on goatskin, fitted thinly: the only open rubab found is the 2 s solo opening belamalta10's '3 continents' (Freesound 513846, CC0 as uploaded -- GarageBand's loop, so measure only; examples/rabab_fit.py). Its seven notes give the colour (the fundamental weak, h2-h7 15-30 dB over it, the top falling from 2.5 kHz; 0.8 dB rms over 12 bands), the decay (24 dB/s, the banjo's 19) and the STOP: the plectrum damps each string before the next pluck (1.3-4.4 dB in the 20-35 ms before it, then ~100 dB/s; Ben heard it), so the note-off stops it. The sympathetic strings ASSUMED (the oud's contact model, eight on C's natural scale). At the banjo's level. Ben: 'Sounds good' |
| 105.16 | SC-55 | Gopichant | `Gopichant` | **4** | the ektara of Bengal's Bauls, fitted to Rajobrata's Shantiniketan ektara (Pianobook, 'The Ektara': C3-A3, three strokes, three round-robins, plain and bent; measure only). THE SCOOP from the 45 bent takes: 236 cents under 30-50 ms in, 15 by 0.15 s, deeper the harder (151-276), an exponential in pitch (0.04 s), every pluck. The string from the plain takes (examples/pluck_fit.py --voice=gopichant): nearly harmonic (B 1e-5, the banjo's 8e-5 had stretched the top out of place -- the high notes' missing top Ben heard), plucked a third along (the banjo's 0.12 notched 3-4 kHz on G3-A3), its decay 7 dB/s rms, its colour to 13 kHz 0.7 dB rms. At the banjo's level. Ben: 'Sounds good' |
| 105.24 | SC-55 | Oud | `Oud` | **4** | the Arabic oud, fitted to Andy R. Jordan's notes (Commons, CC BY-SA 3.0) and hammondman's (Freesound, CC0; examples/pluck_fit.py --voice=oud): a plain plucked string struck near its end (the risha), the kanun's structure where the nylon guitar was 30-60 dB short up top, its bowl as a third-octave trim (5.6 -> 2.5 dB rms), its decay from hammondman's long notes; its DOUBLE COURSES measured from each harmonic's beating (examples/course_sympathy.py: 4-9 cents, the second string ~6 dB under), and its other open courses KICKED through the bridge (contact, the Arab tuning C2 F2 A2 D3 G3 C4: the A and D courses 26-42 dB under the note, the D ~12 cents sharp, the outer four under -45). Ben: 'Sounds good' |

## Bank 127: the MT-32 set

GS's bank 127 is the MT-32's 128 tones on the GS programs (SC-55 owner's
manual p.69), for a file that selects it. Each plays its nearest voice
here (patch_map.MT32_SET) at that voice's level, rated as that voice.

| program.127 | MT-32 tone | plays | |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.127 | Acou Piano 1 | GM 0 Acoustic Grand Piano | **4** |
| 1.127 | Acou Piano 2 | GM 1 Bright Acoustic Piano | **4** |
| 2.127 | Acou Piano 3 | GM 1 Bright Acoustic Piano | **4** |
| 3.127 | Elec Piano 1 | GM 4 Electric Piano 1 | **4** |
| 4.127 | Elec Piano 2 | GM 5 Electric Piano 2 | **4** |
| 5.127 | Elec Piano 3 | GM 4 Electric Piano 1 | **4** |
| 6.127 | Elec Piano 4 | GM 5 Electric Piano 2 | **4** |
| 7.127 | Honkytonk | GM 3 Honky-tonk Piano | **3** |
| 8.127 | Elec Org 1 | GM 16 Drawbar Organ | **4** |

## Sound Canvas: the GS maps

Each Sound Canvas's map of tones (the SC-55's own owner's manual p.68; the SC-55mkII,
SC-88 and SC-88Pro maps from the SC-88Pro manual's list), against ours. A tone is built
when we play it, as a program or a variation, and rated as the tables above rate it.
A later map can put another tone under a number we built; that one is not counted.

| map | tones | built | | 4 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 0 | to do |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SC-55 | 214 | 214 | 100% | 171 | 40 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| SC-55mkII | 226 | 226 | 100% | 178 | 48 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| SC-88 | 418 | 418 | 100% | 285 | 130 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| SC-88Pro | 774 | 436 | 56% | 294 | 139 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 338 (11 another tone) |
| SC-8850 | 1262 | 434 | 34% | 292 | 139 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 828 (13 another tone) |

A file selects a map with bank select LSB (CC32: 1 SC-55, 2 SC-88, 3 SC-88Pro, 4 SC-8850; none, ours). Where a map holds another tone under a number, it is built as its own: SC-55 3.8 HonkyTonk w (3), SC-55 4.16 E.Piano 1v (4), SC-55 5.16 E.Piano 2v (4), SC-55 62.16 Analog Brs1 (3), SC-55 63.16 Analog Brs2 (3), SC-88 4.8 St.Soft EP (4), SC-88 39.8 Beef FMBass (3).

### The SC-88 map's 0 still to build, by what they take


## Effects: the Sound Canvas's

Rated on the voices' scale: 4 validated against a target (a recording of the device, or the hardware's output of a file judged against ours by ear), 3 validated semantically by ear, 2 from theory (Roland's tables, our models), 1 a stand-in, 0 not built (the sound passes dry). The reverb is the module's way of giving the music a space, and our physical rooms are how we do that job: it is rated on whether the space is the one the arranger asked for. The plan maps each insertion type onto the physical model we have where one exists (Rotary the Leslie, Overdrive/Distortion the valve amp and cabinet, the choruses chorus.py, the 3D types our room and head).

| effect | | notes |
|---|---|---|
| Reverb (system) | **3** | the macro picks one of our physical rooms by what it is for (Room 1-3 studio, chamber, chapel; Hall 1 CHAMBER -- validated against Tokei on the SC-88Pro, a 4 for Hall 1: boomy in our hall, right in the chamber (Ben); Hall 2 church), its level and time scale the tail from the macro's own preset; CC91 a distance. Character, Pre-LPF, pre-delay, the delay macros' echo and a per-drum reverb send not yet |
| Chorus (system) | **3** | chorus.gs_copies: Roland's eight macros, a line swept by one LFO, read at two outputs in opposite phase, fed back; laws GM 2's (rate, depth, feedback), the delay's chosen (0.2 ms a step); the wet share held as CC93's. To reverb / to delay, per-drum chorus sends, not yet |
| Delay (system) | **3** | gsdelay.py: Roland's three taps, time table, ten macros, feedback through the center; CC94 (in a GS file) and the drum keys' sends; level laws chosen (linear over 127). Delay to reverb not yet a send of its own |
| EQ (system) | **2** | the SC-88's two shelves at 200/400 Hz and 3/6 kHz, per part on/off, first-order (the order chosen) |

### Insertion effects (EFX, the SC-88Pro's 64 types)

Read per note at its onset (the files automate the level, the mix, the sends); a part assigned to a type not built plays dry.

| type | | notes |
|---|---|---|
| Stereo-EQ | **3** | two shelves at their own corners and two peaking mids (gsefx.eq_gain), every partial; heard on sysex_01 (Ben: "These all sound great") |
| Spectrum | **3** | eight fixed peaking bands at the manual's frequencies, each partial's gain at its own; heard on a piano |
| Enhancer | **3** | efxbus: what lies above 2 kHz saturated, its harmonics mixed back by Mix; heard on an EP |
| Humanizer | **3** | efxfilter: three formants of its vowel (a/i/u/e/o), gliding at its Accel; its drive the valve's gain stage; heard on a Moog lead |
| Overdrive | **3** | the OD a gain stage in front of the part's valve (tubeamp, its gains multiplying), its output makeup, the 1x12 cabinet; low/high gains after it. Amp Type (Small/BltIn/2-/3-Stack) one speaker for all; heard in the OD -> Chorus/Flanger/Delay demos (Ben, 2026-10-21: those sound great) |
| Distortion | **3** | as Overdrive, with more gain; heard on storm_pro (kathie, SC-88Pro), its part 8 clean electric guitar at Drive 70 (Ben: sounds good) |
| Phaser | **3** | efxfilter: four all-passes at the swept corner, its resonance and mix, on every partial (the lobe kernel's filter_grid); heard on our riff |
| Auto Wah | **3** | efxfilter: a resonant LPF/BPF swept up (or down) from its Manual by its LFO, and opened by its Sens (the part's notes' envelope); heard on our riff and a Clav (Ben: "sounds great") |
| Rotary | **3** | our Leslie at the type's rotor speeds, switched by Speed, Accl through our measured spin-ups, the rotors' levels -- RECORDED as Roland's is: two mics round the cabinet (Separate, 0-180 degrees), each to its speaker with its own swing and Doppler; heard on our Rhodes riff, mono and then with its two mics (Ben: "Those sound awesome", "Sounds great") |
| Stereo Flanger | **3** | the chorus device with a flanger's swing, Roland's pre-delay and feedback, its outputs apart by its Phase; heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| Step Flanger | **3** | efxfilter: the flanger's comb, its line swept in steps; heard on our riff |
| Tremolo | **3** | the tremolo pass, Roland's five waves as Fourier series, the depth dipping the level; heard on our riff |
| Auto Pan | **3** | the same across the ears; heard on our riff |
| Compressor | **3** | efxbus: a level-following gain at the output's scale, 10-40 dB down at 8:1, attack 1-50 ms (chosen); heard on our guitar riff |
| Limiter | **3** | efxbus: the compressor at its Threshold and Ratio, Release 10 ms-1 s (chosen); heard on our guitar riff |
| Hexa Chorus | **3** | six voices spread in delay, depth and pan by its Dev parameters (0.5 ms a Pre Dly Dev step, chosen); heard in its demo (Ben, 2026-10-21: these all sound great) |
| Tremolo Chorus | **3** | the chorus and the tremolo pass (half depth, chosen); heard in its demo (Ben, 2026-10-21: these all sound great) |
| Stereo Chorus | **3** | as Space D, its Phase and pre-filter (the LPF; its HPF not built); heard in its demo (Ben, 2026-10-21: these all sound great) |
| Space D | **3** | chorus.gs_copies, two outputs half a turn apart, pre-delay and rate from Roland's tables, Balance the share; heard on SENSATIO |
| 3D Chorus | **3** | the chorus's outputs at 60 degrees, past the speakers; heard in its demo (Ben, 2026-10-21: these all sound great) |
| Stereo Delay | **3** | gsdelay: left and right taps each looping at its own period, feedback (cross for ping-pong), phase inversion, HF damping; heard on our riff (Ben: "They sound great") |
| Mod Delay | **3** | the Stereo Delay with its modulation as the repeats' vibrato; heard on our riff |
| 3 Tap Delay | **3** | centre, left and right taps, the centre's loop; heard on our riff |
| 4 Tap Delay | **3** | four taps spread left to right (chosen); heard on our riff |
| Tm Ctrl Delay | **3** | one tap at its time, placed by its pan (its time's glide under control not built); heard on our riff |
| Reverb | **3** | efxbus: a decaying noise response per ear, its highs dying to HF Damp, six types by density, tail and early reflections (chosen); heard on an EP |
| Gate Reverb | **3** | efxbus: a noise burst Gate Time long convolved with the part -- Norm, Reverse, the two Sweeps; heard on Melodic Tom |
| 3D Delay | **3** | centre tap and two at 60 degrees; heard on our riff |
| 2 Pitch Shifter | **3** | two shifted voices as gsdelay taps (Coarse, Fine, delay, pan, L.Bal); heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| Fb P.Shifter | **3** | a shifted tap whose repeats shift again round the loop; heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| 3D Auto | **3** | the same, turning at its Speed; each note where its onset finds it; heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| 3D Manual | **3** | the part at the type's Azimuth, past the speakers and behind (tonelib pan_angle); heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| Lo-Fi 1 | **3** | efxbus, the part's own bus: held to a lower rate, quantised to fewer bits (Type 1-9, chosen), its pre and post low-passes; heard on an EP and sysex_01 (Ben: "sounds great") |
| Lo-Fi 2 | **3** | efxbus: its rate and bits, a filter after, and noises heard through silence -- a radio's detune and static, white/pink, a record's crackle (33/45/78 rpm), mains hum; heard on an EP (Ben: "sounds great") |
| OD -> Chorus | **3** | the valve's gain stage and guitar cabinet, then the chorus (gs_copies); heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| OD -> Flanger | **3** | the same with the flanger; heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| OD -> Delay | **3** | the same with a gsdelay tap; its dry and effect by Bal; heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| DS -> Chorus | **3** | as OD -> Chorus with the distortion's gain; heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| DS -> Flanger | **3** | as OD -> Flanger with the distortion's gain; heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| DS -> Delay | **3** | as OD -> Delay with the distortion's gain; heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| EH -> Chorus | **3** | the chorus's copies, then the Enhancer on the part's bus (after them: the other way about); heard on an EP (Ben: "sounds great") |
| EH -> Flanger | **3** | the flanger, then the Enhancer's bus; heard on a guitar |
| EH -> Delay | **3** | a delay tap, then the Enhancer's bus; heard on an EP |
| Cho -> Delay | **3** | the chorus and a delay tap (our passes run the delay first); heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| FL -> Delay | **3** | the flanger and a delay tap; heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| Cho -> Flanger | **3** | the chorus's copies, then a moving comb (efxfilter) on every row, the copies' too: in its order; heard on strings |
| Rotary Multi | **3** | the Rotary with its OD (the valve) and EQ before the rotors; heard in its demo (Ben, 2026-10-21: those sound awesome) |
| GTR Multi 1 | **3** | compressor (decay slowed, its Attack the onset's rise), OD and amp, chorus/flanger (chorus.gs_copies), its delay (gsdelay); every type at Roland's own defaults (gs_efx_params); heard in its demo, +20.6 dB over the dry guitar at the defaults (Ben, 2026-10-22: sounds good; louder, but not crazy loud) |
| GTR Multi 2 | **4** | compressor, OD and amp, 3-band EQ after the valve, chorus/flanger -- VALIDATED against Tokei on the SC-88Pro for the settings it uses (Ben: Crazy Train's crunch, a little less smashed; the makeup lowered by ear); Amp Type and the compressor's Attack not yet built |
| GTR Multi 3 | **3** | as GTR Multi 1, with its wah (efxfilter: a resonant LPF/BPF at its pedal, where the file moves it); heard on our guitar riff (Ben: "Those all sound great") |
| Clean Gt Multi 1 | **3** | compressor, EQ, chorus/flanger and delay, as the guitar multis'; heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| Clean Gt Multi 2 | **3** | an auto wah (efxfilter), the EQ, chorus/flanger and its delay; heard on a guitar |
| Bass Multi | **3** | compressor, OD, EQ, chorus/flanger, the 4x10 bass cabinet; heard on our riff (Ben: "sound great") |
| Rhodes Multi | **3** | Enhancer (the bus), phaser (efxfilter), chorus/flanger, tremolo/pan; heard on an EP |
| Keyboard Multi | **3** | ring modulator (each partial's f +- Hz sidebands as gsdelay taps), EQ, pitch shifter, phaser, delay -- the series unrolled into a tap per path; heard on an EP |
| Cho / Delay | **3** | TWO BRANCHES: the part's rows the chorus's, a copy on its virtual channel the delay's, each at its own Level and Pan (a gain across the ears; the chorus's copies keep its +-30 degree outputs); heard (Ben: "They all sound great") |
| FL / Delay | **3** | the flanger's branch and the delay's, in parallel; heard (Ben: "They all sound great") |
| Cho / Flanger | **3** | the chorus's branch and the flanger's, in parallel; heard (Ben: "They all sound great") |
| OD1 / OD2 | **3** | two OD branches, each the valve's gain stage and cabinet at its own drive, level and pan; heard (Ben: "They all sound great") |
| OD / Rotary | **3** | an OD branch beside a Leslie branch (the voice's own valve in both); heard (Ben: "They all sound great") |
| OD / Phaser | **3** | an OD branch beside a phaser branch (efxfilter); heard (Ben: "They all sound great") |
| OD / AutoWah | **3** | an OD branch beside an auto wah branch, its Sens the part's notes; heard (Ben: "They all sound great") |
| PH / Rotary | **3** | a phaser branch beside a Leslie branch; heard (Ben: "They all sound great") |
| PH / AutoWah | **3** | a phaser branch beside an auto wah branch; heard (Ben: "They all sound great") |

## Roland GS SysEx: the address map

What the file renderer reads of Roland's GS map (VE-GS Pro MIDI Implementation; the SC-88Pro manual for the insertion effects). **Yes** read and applied; **Partial** read, with its limit; **No** not read. Live reads the part parameters, the rhythm part, the master settings and the reset, on port A.

51 rows: 24 read, 8 partly, 19 not.

### System

| address | parameter | | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `40 00 7F, 00 00 7F` | GS Reset, System Mode Set | **Yes** | back to power-on: banks, programs, controllers, tunings, effects, drum edits, the part settings below |
| `40 00 00-05` | Master tune, volume, key shift | **Yes** | the file's timeline; live too |
| `40 00 06` | Master pan | **No** |  |

### Reverb, chorus, delay, EQ (40 01, 40 02)

| address | parameter | | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `40 01 30` | Reverb macro | **Partial** | the arranger's space onto one of our rooms (Room 1 the studio, Hall 2 the church); read before the first note |
| `40 01 33-34` | Reverb level, time | **Partial** | onto the room's tail; before the first note |
| `40 01 31-32, 35, 37` | Reverb character, pre-LPF, feedback, pre-delay | **No** |  |
| `40 01 38-3E` | Chorus macro and parameters | **Partial** | the GS chorus device; before the first note |
| `40 01 3F-40` | Chorus send to reverb, delay | **No** | parsed, not used |
| `40 01 50-59` | Delay macro and parameters | **Partial** | the GS delay; before the first note |
| `40 01 5A` | Delay send to reverb | **No** | parsed, not used |
| `40 02 00-03` | Master EQ, low and high | **Partial** | each partial at its frequency; before the first note |

### Insertion effect (40 03)

| address | parameter | | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `40 03 00-01` | Type | **Yes** | 64 of 64 built: the filters and EQs, the drives and guitar/bass/keyboard multis, the choruses and flangers, the delays, Rotary, Tremolo, Auto Pan, the 3D types, the pitch shifters, the moving filters (efxfilter), a part's own bus (Lo-Fi, the dynamics, Enhancer, the reverbs: efxbus), the series pairs and the parallel ones (branch B on a virtual channel); Thru plays dry |
| `40 03 03-16` | Parameters 1-20 | **Partial** | each type at Roland's own defaults (the data table) until the file sends one; drive and level follow the song, the EQ and chorus/flanger settings are the part's first note's; Amp Type not read |
| `40 03 17, 19` | Send to reverb, delay | **Yes** | per note |
| `40 03 18` | Send to chorus | **No** | parsed, not used |
| `40 03 1B-1E` | Control sources and depths | **Yes** | CC1-95, aftertouch or bend onto each type's + and # parameter, Roland's law (setting + controller x depth of the range); a file's at each note's onset, live's at once; the live panel learns the knob |
| `40 4x 22` | Part assign | **Yes** | ports A and B |

### Part (40 1x)

| address | parameter | | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `40 1x 00` | Tone number | **Yes** | as CC0 and program change |
| `40 1x 02-12` | Rx channel and switches | **No** |  |
| `40 1x 13` | Mono/poly | **Yes** | as CC126/127 |
| `40 1x 14` | Assign mode | **No** |  |
| `40 1x 15` | Use for rhythm part | **Yes** |  |
| `40 1x 16` | Pitch key shift | **Partial** | as RPN 2: replaces the file's own rather than adding to it |
| `40 1x 17-18` | Pitch offset fine (Hz) | **No** |  |
| `40 1x 19, 1C, 2C` | Part level, pan, delay send | **Yes** | as CC7, CC10 (random as centre), CC94 |
| `40 1x 1A-1B` | Velocity sense depth, offset | **Yes** | at each note-on |
| `40 1x 1D-1E` | Keyboard range | **Yes** | notes outside it do not sound |
| `40 1x 1F-20` | CC1/CC2 controller numbers | **No** |  |
| `40 1x 21-22` | Chorus, reverb send | **Yes** | as CC93, CC91 -- each note's onset finds its own (the sends follow the song) |
| `40 1x 23-24` | Rx bank select | **No** |  |
| `40 1x 2A` | Pitch fine tune | **Yes** | as RPN 1 |
| `40 1x 30-37` | Tone Modify 1-8 | **Yes** | as the sound controllers CC71-78 (= the NRPNs) |
| `40 1x 40-4B` | Scale tuning | **Yes** | through the scale/octave tuning |
| `40 2x 04` | Mod LFO1 pitch depth | **Yes** | as RPN 5, the mod wheel's vibrato depth |
| `40 2x 10` | Bend pitch control | **Yes** | as RPN 0, the bend range |
| `40 2x (the rest)` | Mod, bend, aftertouch, CC1/CC2 onto TVF, TVA, LFOs | **No** | a Sound Canvas's own circuits, which our voices are not built from |
| `40 4x 00-01` | Tone map number | **No** | the map comes from CC32 |
| `40 4x 20` | EQ switch | **Partial** | before the first note |

### Drum setup (41) and drum NRPNs

| address | parameter | | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `41 m1 / NRPN 18` | Play note, pitch | **Yes** |  |
| `41 m2 / NRPN 1A` | Level | **Yes** |  |
| `41 m4 / NRPN 1C` | Pan | **Yes** | a seat inside the kit, turned by the part's pan |
| `41 m8` | Rx note on | **Yes** |  |
| `41 m9 / NRPN 1F` | Delay send | **Yes** |  |
| `41 m3` | Assign group | **No** | parsed, not used; the kits' own choke groups apply |
| `41 m5-m6 / NRPN 1D-1E` | Reverb, chorus send | **No** | parsed, not used |
| `41 m7` | Rx note off | **No** | parsed, not used |

### User sets, display, dumps

| address | parameter | | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `21 d1, d2, d4, d8, dB, dC` | User drum set: note, level, pan, Rx note on, source set and note | **Yes** |  |
| `21 (the rest)` | User drum set: group, sends, Rx note off, source map | **No** |  |
| `20, 22-27` | User tones, user EFX, user patches | **No** |  |
| `45 ...` | Display text and pages | **No** | not sound |
| `bulk dumps` | 48, 49, ... | **No** |  |


## The class tree

418 classes.

Every voice class a program or a drum note is routed to, under its physical
base (the first base that is itself a voice class; mixins in brackets). After
each: the GM programs (`GM n`), percussion notes (`n`) and drum-set notes
(`set k n`) that use it, with their ratings.
